





Around higher-order Godbersen conjectures










 



  // Restore the saved color scheme preference, or
  // enact the browser preference if "automatic", 
  // without expecting DOM load to have completed.
  //
  // Also restore any saved readingmode and ToC display preferences.
  function initializeReadingPreferences() {
    let saved_theme = localStorage.getItem("ar5iv_theme") || "automatic";
    if (saved_theme === "automatic") {
      if (window.matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)").matches) {
        saved_theme = "dark";
      }
    }
    if (saved_theme == "dark") {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-theme", "dark");
    } else {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-theme", "light");
    }

    const tocDisplay = localStorage.getItem('arxiv_html_paper_toc_display');
    if (tocDisplay) {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-toc-display", tocDisplay);
    }
    const readingMode = localStorage.getItem('arxiv_html_paper_reading_mode');
    if (readingMode) {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-reading-mode", readingMode);
    }
  }
  // Run as soon as JS starts, to minimize repainting
  initializeReadingPreferences();
















  /* Reading mode hides the chrome and the empty .arxiv-html-header marker: the pinned JS reads
     the marker's display to know which way to toggle. The marker draws no box. */
  .arxiv-html-header { display: contents; }
  html[data-reading-mode="enabled"] :is(.arxiv-html-site-chrome, .arxiv-html-header, .ds-site-footer) { display: none; }
  /* The paper controls' icons are blocks, so they sit centred (the pinned JS shows the theme
     icon by making its span a block). */
  .arxiv-html-site-chrome .ds-site-header-tools svg { display: block; width: 18px; height: 18px; flex-shrink: 0; fill: currentColor; }
  /* ar5iv dims every image in dark mode and puts it on a white wash, for paper figures;
     arXiv's own render as they are (the logo on the bar, the funder logos on their white
     chips). Important: ar5iv's dark rules are unlayered, as specific as
     :root:not([data-theme]) img:not(.ltx_no_dark_filter). */
  :is(.ds-site-header, .ds-site-footer) img { filter: none !important; }
  .ds-site-header img { background-color: transparent !important; }

  /* Wide (the theme's 1280px): the theme lays the body out as a grid whose full-width top rows
     were the band's and its sticky reader bar's. The band takes the first and scrolls away; the
     header, the paper's bar (no site navigation, the paper's controls), takes the second and
     stays in view, the one sticky bar, with the anchor clearance that requires (DESIGN-POLICIES).
     The controls show their names. */
  @media (min-width: 1280px) {
    html { scroll-padding-top: 80px; }
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome { display: contents; }
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome > #ds-announcement { grid-area: announce; }
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome > .ds-site-header { grid-area: header; position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 3; }
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome .tool-icon { display: none; }
  }
  /* Narrow: the header scrolls away, and the controls show icons (names kept for screen
     readers; Why HTML? goes). The theme's contents panel is fixed below where its old sticky
     bar was, and hidden only once the reader has closed it, so until then it is an invisible box
     over the header and the paper: it shows only while open, under the chrome, in its place in
     the page. The paper's footer (build logs, reporting instructions) keeps the text's side
     inset; the theme gives it none, so it met the screen edges. */
  @media (max-width: 1279px) {
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome .tool-wide { display: none; }
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome .tool-label { position: absolute; width: 1px; height: 1px; overflow: hidden; clip: rect(0 0 0 0); white-space: nowrap; }
    html:not([data-toc-display="block"]) .ltx_page_navbar { display: none; }
    .ltx_page_navbar { position: absolute; top: auto; }
    .arxiv-html-footer { padding-inline: 1rem; }
  }

arXiv:2609.08612v2 [math.MG] 17 Sep 2026






Around higher-order Godbersen conjectures
Thanks: 
FF was supported by Charles University grants PRIMUS/24/SCI/009 and GAUK/34126. JK was supported by Charles University grants PRIMUS/24/SCI/009 and UNCE/24/SCI/022.






Filip Fryš



 and 


Jan Kotrbatý



Address: 
Charles University, Faculty of Mathematics and Physics, Mathematical Institute of Charles University, Sokolovská 49/83, 186 00 Prague, Czechia



Email address: 
filip.frys@matfyz.cuni.cz




Email address: 
kotrbaty@karlin.mff.cuni.cz




Date: September 17, 2026



Abstract.

    

We consider two higher-order generalizations of the Godbersen conjecture for mixed volumes of convex bodies, which were first proposed by Schneider in 2000. First, we establish the conjectures in several special cases, in particular in low dimensions. Second, we prove that certain consequences of the conjectures hold. More precisely, we define a higher-order version of the unbalanced difference body and prove related weighted inequalities, generalizing previous results of Artstein-Avidan and Putterman. Finally, we introduce higher-order analogs of unbalanced joins of convex bodies considered by Artstein-Avidan, Einhorn, Florentin, and Ostrover, prove the corresponding volume bounds, and propose conjectures that interpolate between the higher-order Godbersen conjectures and a conjecture due to Fáry and Rédei.

  



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
52A40, 52A39





1. 
Introduction





The Godbersen conjecture asserts that the geometric invariants associated with a convex body in Euclidean space via mixed volumes with the opposite body are maximized by simplices. Dating back to the 1938 thesis of C. Godbersen 
[
5
]
, it has been one of the longest-standing open problems in convex geometry, and until very recently, only special cases and consequences of the conjectured inequality have been known.






A notable example of such a consequence is the classical Rogers–Shephard inequality 
[

1.1. 
Our results





In this paper, motivated by the previous developments in the classical case 
p
=
1

, we provide additional evidence for validity of the higher-order Godbersen conjectures. More precisely, we verify Conjectures

Special cases of the higher-order Godbersen conjectures

Third, we show that the higher-order difference body of a product body is the product of higher-order difference bodies and conclude that the validity of both conjectures is stable under the operation of taking cartesian products.

Let us emphasize that throughout the article the Lebesgue measure and the mixed volume will be considered on Euclidean spaces of various dimensions. We believe it should always be clear from the context what the dimension is.

Instructions for reporting errors

    
We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile
      support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the
      methods listed below:

    

      
Click the "Report Issue" 
(

            

          
)
 button, located in the page header.

    

    
Tip:
 You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.

    
Our team has already identified 
the following issues
. We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we
      may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
      should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
      all.

    
Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a 
list of packages that need conversion
, and welcome 
developer contributions
.
